Our analysis reveals that, despite substantial progress in self-improvement, the surveyed literature does not yet demonstrate Physical RSI as defined by our framework. We identify research priorities in reliable feedback, causal attribution, safety preservation, and benchmarking, outlining a path toward robots that autonomously improve how they learn while ensuring safety. The GitHub repository for Physical RSI is available at this https URL.

</details>

### 355. LIBERO-RIG: A Hardware-Setup Robustness Benchmark for Vision-Language-Action Models

📄 [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18843)　📅 2026-10

**关键词**：`VLA`、`hardware robustness`、`benchmark`、`deployment`

👤 **作者**：Andrea Yaoyun Cui、…、Matthew Tancik

- 🎯 **研究动机**：机器人策略应跨部署环境泛化——包括机器人自身的变化（相机被撞重装/标度漂移/控制器变速/末端垫磨损）
- 🔬 **研究方法**：LIBERO-RIG：固定全部 LIBERO 任务只扰动硬件与设置——11 轴×5 严重度=210 条件；九个 VLA×每条件 500 回合+严格统计检验
- 📌 **结论**：汇总成功率 0.932→0.767（中度）→0.509（最高）；退化跨扰动轴与动作接口差异巨大，视觉扰动训练不能缓解不改相机图像的故障——VLA 的硬件设置鲁棒性基准

<details>
<summary>📝 展开完整英文摘要（Abstract）</summary>

Robot policies are expected to generalize across deployment environments, including variations in the robot itself: cameras are bumped and remounted, calibrations drift, controllers run at different rates, and end-effector pads wear down. We present LIBERO-RIG, a benchmark that perturbs only the robot's hardware and setup while holding every LIBERO task fixed. The benchmark spans eleven perturbation axes, including camera mounting and optics, camera-to-arm calibration, actuation gain, control rate, proprioceptive noise, base pose, table height, and gripper pads. Each axis has five severity levels, yielding 210 conditions across the four LIBERO suites. We evaluate nine recent vision-language-action models using 500 episodes per condition and substantiate each finding with rigorous statistical tests. Pooled success falls from 0.932 to 0.767 at the middle severity and 0.509 at the highest severity. Performance degradation varies substantially across perturbation axes and action interfaces, and training on visual perturbations does not mitigate faults that leave camera images unchanged. We release the benchmark together with manifests specifying the fixed perturbation parameters for each episode.

</details>

### 356. The Last Remote Sensing Model Built by Humans? Casting Any Earth Observation Task as Recursive Self-Improvement

📄 [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18845)　📅 2026-10

**关键词**：`earth observation`、`recursive self-improvement`、`harness+verifier`、`agent-built models`

👤 **作者**：Sijie Zhao、…、Naoto Yokoya

- 🎯 **研究动机**：EO 模型仍靠人手工构建（专家设计配方并对基准调优）——这个循环能否交给 agent
- 🔬 **研究方法**：任何可验证任务= harness（agent 可用什么）+verifier（什么算成功）；EO 任务可经标注基准/独立传感器/物理模型/同地重复观测验证，通用 coding agent 在自产输出上递归改进模型
- 📌 **结论**：四个 EO 基准（光学/SAR/多光谱时序）上 agent 建的模型超 SOTA 并以约 1% 训练标签保持多数精度——人设计 harness 与 verifier、agent 造模型的新范式（RSI 的领域实例）

<details>
<summary>📝 展开完整英文摘要（Abstract）</summary>

Earth observation (EO) models are still built by hand. For each new task, experts design a training recipe and tune it against a benchmark. We show that this loop can be handed to an agent. Any verifiable task can be cast as a harness and a verifier, where the harness defines what the agent may use and the verifier defines what counts as success. Every EO task can be made verifiable, through labeled benchmarks, independent sensors, physical models or the repeated observation of the same place. Given only these two components, a general-purpose coding agent builds the EO model and improves it recursively on its own outputs. Settings long treated as separate research problems, from learning with a handful of labels to adapting across domains, become choices of harness and verifier. On four EO benchmarks spanning optical, SAR and multispectral time-series imagery, agent-built models surpass the state of the art and keep most of that accuracy from about 1% of the training labels. This establishes a new paradigm for EO, in which people design harnesses and verifiers and agents build the models, so EO models improve as agents do.

</details>

### 357. AutoAgent0: An Agentic Runtime for Safe Closed-Loop Driving

📄 [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18866)　📅 2026-10

**关键词**：`closed-loop driving`、`agentic runtime`、`action verification`、`recovery`

👤 **作者**：Jiageng Liu、…、Jiaqi Ma

- 🎯 **研究动机**：安全自动驾驶需要动态环境中可靠的闭环执行——端到端策略的累积闭环误差可把车辆推入同策略难以恢复的状态
- 🔬 **研究方法**：AutoAgent0：agentic 运行时增强既有驾驶专家——独立检测模块构建场景上下文+规则验证器执行前检查轨迹+不可靠时激活恢复规划（动作原语构造替代提案并用验证反馈迭代精修）
- 📌 **结论**：Fail2Drive/NavSafe-∞ 上长尾与安全关键场景的闭环鲁棒性优于单专家执行——测试时执行的驾驶安全（具身 harness 的运行时守门）

<details>
<summary>📝 展开完整英文摘要（Abstract）</summary>

Safe autonomous driving requires more than accurate planning at a single frame: it demands reliable closed-loop execution in dynamic environments. Although end-to-end driving policies have advanced rapidly, the accumulated closed-loop errors, especially in unseen scenarios, can push vehicles into states from which the same policy struggles to recover. We address the challenge by treating closed-loop driving safety as a test-time execution problem. We introduce AutoAgent0, an agentic runtime and harness framework that augments existing driving experts with complementary detection skills, rule-based action verification, and feedback-driven recovery. To address inaccurate scene perception, an independent detection module constructs scene context using complementary perception skills, and a rule-based verifier checks proposed trajectories against this context before execution. When the nominal trajectories are unreliable, the orchestrator of the runtime activates Recovery Planning, where an agentic loop constructs alternative action proposals from action primitives and iteratively refines them using verification feedback. This design combines independent scene assessment with feasibility checks and online recovery, addressing both perception errors and the limited recovery capabilities of individual experts. Evaluations on Fail2Drive and NavSafe-$\infty$ demonstrate improved closed-loop robustness over single-expert execution in long-tail and safety-critical scenarios. These results demonstrate that runtime supervision can improve closed-loop robustness across multiple driving experts without retraining the underlying policies.

</details>

### 358. EmbodiedEvo: Self-improving Coding Agents for Physical Intelligence

📄 [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18867)　📅 2026-10

**关键词**：`embodied coding agent`、`self-improvement`、`skills and lessons`、`physical intelligence`

👤 **作者**：Wenyi Li、…、Qinhuai Na

- 🎯 **研究动机**：把通用 agent 带入物理世界需要可执行的物理智能表示——代码使控制逻辑显式可复用且可作 agent 解决任务的工作区
- 🔬 **研究方法**：EmbodiedEvo：coding agent 在单个 Playground 场景经反馈演化可复用 Skills 与 Lessons 后冻结 harness 用于多样新场景；SWE 式循环（自主检查状态/改执行代码/按反馈选下一步）
- 📌 **结论**：RoboDojo-Sim 均分 44.48/成功率 37.82% 超全部官方验证结果；强模型经验提升弱执行器（Astra 经验使 Sol/Luna +8.86/+5.50pp）——具身自改进的经验复用与边界（精细接触控制仍难）

<details>
<summary>📝 展开完整英文摘要（Abstract）</summary>

Bringing general-purpose agents into the physical world calls for an executable representation of physical intelligence. Compared with vision-language-action (VLA) models, which map observations and language instructions directly to actions, code makes control logic explicit and reusable. Code can serve not only as a policy representation but also as the workspace in which the agent solves a task. Rather than following a prescribed workflow of planning, execution, and verification, the agent can autonomously inspect the state, revise and execute code, and use feedback to choose the next step. The success of this SWE-style loop in digital environments motivates its extension to physical tasks. Yet physical execution remains prone to recurring failures. To retain and refine experience for later scenes, we introduce EmbodiedEvo, where a coding agent evolves reusable Skills and Lessons through feedback on a single Playground scene, then freezes the resulting harness for diverse new scenes. On RoboDojo-Sim, EmbodiedEvo achieves an average score of 44.48 and a success rate of 37.82\%, surpassing all officially verified results. Paired tests show the largest gains on memory-dependent tasks and successful reuse of coordinated procedures, while fine contact control remains difficult. Experience from stronger models also improves weaker executors: Astra's experience raises Sol and Luna's success rates by 8.86 and 5.50 percentage points over no experience. Compared with raw interaction history, Skills and Lessons achieve comparable task performance at 53.0\% lower execution cost.

</details>
